Robot foundation models provide initial capabilities by translating visual and language understanding into physical action [3, 4, 11, 20, 44], but these initial capabilities do not automatically yield reliable execution [5, 19, 31]. Variations in object properties, contact conditions and individual robots expose gaps in these capabilities, particularly when errors accumulate over multi-stage tasks [21, 43]. At the same time, robots operating in the real world generate experience that can support further policy improvement [1, 6]. As deployment expands to larger fleets, parallel execution provides growing source of physical experience [16, 17, 22, 32, 36]. The question is how to turn this growing experience into more reliable task execution during deployment. central constraint is the human effort required to supply corrective actions during deployment. Expert demonstrations and corrective interventions have proved useful for improving policies during real-world execution [1, 13, 18, 24, 25, 34]. Recent fleet-learning systems combine parallel collection with human supervision to connect robot execution and policy updates [14, 32, 36]. When learning depends on new expert actions, teleoperators must remain available to monitor execution, recognize failures and take control to provide appropriate corrections. Text:"Self-Evolving Coding Agents via Harness-Policy Optimization Zhepei Wei1,2, Wenlin Yao1, Baolin Peng1, Danqing Wang3, Qianhui Wu1 Hao Cheng1, Yu Meng2, Jianfeng Gao1 1Microsoft Research 3Carnegie Mellon University 2University of Virginia https://github.com/MSR-Orchard/Self-HPO https://huggingface.co/self-hpo Abstract Large language model (LLM) agents have shown promising potential in coding tasks, and their performance is jointly determined by the policy model and the harness that mediates their interaction with the environment. Indeed, harness and policy are mutually reinforcing and evolving only one component is suboptimala harness tuned for one checkpoint may not target the failure modes of an updated policy, while policy learning remains limited to the trajectories elicited by fixed harness. In light of this, we propose Self-HPO, recursive self-improving framework for coding agents that alternates harness and policy optimization, using the evolving policy as its own harness proposer. Specifically, in each round, the current policy first analyzes its own failures and proposes new harness candidates (H-step). The policy is then updated through reinforcement learning (RL) under the selected harness (P-step), and hard tasks identified during training form the next harness search set, keeping harness optimization targeted to the policys evolving failure modes. Across three coding benchmarks, Self-HPO consistently outperforms harness-only and policy-only baselines, improving Qwen3.5-35B-A3Bs average resolve rate from 57.8% to 64.8%, with gains generalizing to out-of-distribution settings. Further analysis shows that both optimized components retain gains even when paired with their base counterparts, while their joint gain exceeds the sum of their individual gains. Self-HPO also improves reliability (pass@1) and expands the capability frontier (pass@k) over the base agent. Additionally, we show that with stronger external model (e.g., Opus 4.8) as the harness proposer,"

Text:"CoworkEnv 2026-10-16 CoworkEnv: Synthesizing Training Environments for Long-Horizon Cowork Agents Zhongwei XIE1, Yiming DU2, Jiaxin BAI3, Baixuan XU1, Chunyang LI1, Xiyu REN1, Wei FAN1, Tianshi ZHENG1, Weizhi DU4, Jingqiao YU1, Xingsu LI1, Zhaowei WANG1,*, Yangqiu SONG1 1The Hong Kong University of Science and Technology 2The Chinese University of Hong Kong 3Hong Kong Baptist University 4Independent Researcher {zxiebk, zwanggy}@connect.ust.hk ydu@se.cuhk.edu.hk yqsong@ust.hk *Corresponding author: zwanggy@connect.ust.hk. Code Models Lessons Abstract AI coworkers must coordinate tools, communicate across turns, and act on changing workspace state while respecting workflow constraints. Constructing training experience for these capabilities requires coherent requests, service records, tool interfaces, and grading checks. We present CoworkEnv, capability-guided framework for synthesizing executable training environments for AI coworkers. Workflow requirements guide task construction, and programmatic validation with feedback repair checks generated specifications and grading behavior. Teacher executions yield curated demonstrations for supervised fine-tuning (SFT), while student interactions provide rollouts and rewards for reinforcement learning (RL). Using these resources, we train compact PocketWorker models. For Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct, SFT raises the mean score from 46.6 to 64.1 on Claw-Eval, including 31.9-point gain on Multi-turn tasks. An SFT-only extension to ClawMark demonstrates applicability across coworker benchmarks. We further compare RL training dynamics across configurations that vary reward processing and credit assignment. Improving training rewards provide preliminary evidence that the constructed environments support policy optimization. 1. Introduction AI coworkers are expected to carry out everyday work through sustained interaction with users, tools, and changing workspace. Resolving customer-support request, for example, can require consulting acc"

Text:"Fr echet Forcing: Training Long-Horizon Streaming World Models from Short Clips Yian Zhao, Yu Yan, Hongcan Guo, Ruochong Zheng, Jian Zhang, Jie Chen Abstract Streaming video world models generate the future chunk by chunk, conditioned on their own past and stream of actions. When trained on short clips, they drift once rollout exceeds the training horizon, as prediction errors re-enter the context and compound. common remedy is to train the model on its own rollouts via distribution matching distillation (DMD). For action-conditioned world models, however, the teacher becomes bottleneck: an external video model is agnostic to the actions, whereas the models own pretrained network drifts in the same manner as the student. We observe that long rollout need not match teacher, provided it remains realistic continuation of its own scene that follows the commanded actions. Realism and scene fidelity can be measured directly against real-data statistics, which remain valid at any horizon, while action following is preserved by the models conditional generation loss. Building on this insight, we propose Fréchet Forcing, which fine-tunes world model together with its deployed multi-step sampler under three complementary constraints. Generative anchoring applies the flow-matching loss after self-generated history ending on real data, preserving action-conditioned generation. Context fidelity, Fréchet distance (FD) on latent statistics of the free rollout centred on each clips own ground truth, keeps every rollout faithful to its scene. Reality matching, an FD to real frames in frozen perceptual space, keeps the generated frames realistic. Because all three targets are derived from short real clips without comparing rollout against long recorded future, training requires no teacher, no online critic and no long videos. Fine-tuning MiniWorld-1B, pretrained on 253-frame clips, for at most 36 GPU-hours on 381-frame clips yields rollouts that remain stable beyond 4,000 frames and "

Text:"Fr echet Forcing: Training Long-Horizon Streaming World Models from Short Clips Yian Zhao, Yu Yan, Hongcan Guo, Ruochong Zheng, Jian Zhang, Jie Chen Abstract Streaming video world models generate the future chunk by chunk, conditioned on their own past and stream of actions. When trained on short clips, they drift once rollout exceeds the training horizon, as prediction errors re-enter the context and compound. common remedy is to train the model on its own rollouts via distribution matching distillation (DMD). For action-conditioned world models, however, the teacher becomes bottleneck: an external video model is agnostic to the actions, whereas the models own pretrained network drifts in the same manner as the student. We observe that long rollout need not match teacher, provided it remains realistic continuation of its own scene that follows the commanded actions. Realism and scene fidelity can be measured directly against real-data statistics, which remain valid at any horizon, while action following is preserved by the models conditional generation loss. Building on this insight, we propose Fréchet Forcing, which fine-tunes world model together with its deployed multi-step sampler under three complementary constraints. Generative anchoring applies the flow-matching loss after self-generated history ending on real data, preserving action-conditioned generation. Context fidelity, Fréchet distance (FD) on latent statistics of the free rollout centred on each clips own ground truth, keeps every rollout faithful to its scene. Reality matching, an FD to real frames in frozen perceptual space, keeps the generated frames realistic. Because all three targets are derived from short real clips without comparing rollout against long recorded future, training requires no teacher, no online critic and no long videos. Fine-tuning MiniWorld-1B, pretrained on 253-frame clips, for at most 36 GPU-hours on 381-frame clips yields rollouts that remain stable beyond 4,000 frames and continue to follow their actions in both robot manipulation (DROID) and camera-controlled exploration (Sekai). On DROID, PSNR against forward-backward episode replay stays above 21 dB through frame 4,000, compared with 8.9 dB for the base model. An analysis of the training dynamics further reveals how distribution matching on free rollouts fails and how each design choice prevents these failure modes. Code: https://github.com/zhao-yian/Frechet_Forcing Models: https://huggingface.co/zhaoyian01/Frechet_Forcing Page & Demo: https://zhao-yian.github.io/Frechet_Forcing Figure 1 4,000-frame chunk-by-chunk rollouts from single real frame on two DROID episodes (top four rows) and one Sekai scene (bottom two rows), following the commanded robot or camera trajectory. MiniWorld-1B, pretrained on 253-frame clips, cannot sustain longer rollouts: on DROID, saturated lattice texture spreads from the moving arm within 1,000 frames, and on Sekai the rollout darkens within the first 250 frames and then fades to black. MiniWorld-1B-FF, fine-tuned with Fréchet Forcing on 381-frame clips, stays coherent to frame 4,000, over 10 the fine-tuning horizon. 1 Introduction World models that generate video in streaming fashion, with each chunk conditioned on previously generated chunks and on an action, provide natural substrate for interactive simulation, game engines and embodied agents [1, 5, 6, 10, 15, 34, 45]. However, because they are trained on ground-truth history and deployed autoregressively on their own predictions [4], small errors re-enter the context and compound over time. Rollouts therefore remain coherent for roughly the length of the training clips before drifting, fading to black or dissolving into texture  (Fig. 1)  . This long-horizon drift remains the central obstacle to sustained streaming generation [7, 15, 40]. 2 The prevailing strategy to close this train-test gap is to train the model on its own rollouts. Because self-generated rollout has no paired ground truth beyond the first few seconds, supervision must be distributional, and recent work has largely converged on distribution matching distillation (DMD) [15, 4345], where the difference between frozen teachers score and an online fake score of the student on noised student samples provides the training gradient. State-of-the-art systems reach minute-scale video by extending DMD to longer rollouts, rolling windows and chunk-wise scoring [7, 8, 24, 40]. Teacher-free alternatives instead retain per-sample regression target by conditioning the flow-matching or diffusion loss on history corrupted by the models own errors [9, 20, 41], or by reversing rollouts so that they end on or recover the ground-truth start [14, 29]. For action-conditioned world models, however, the teacher poses fundamental dilemma. An effective teacher must simultaneously model the students domain and action space while remaining more accurate than the student on the states the student visits. large external video model violates the first requirement, as it is agnostic to the action signal and pulls rollouts towards mismatched distribution. Conversely, the world models own pretrained network satisfies the first requirement but violates the second: it is the very model whose rollouts drift, so on long-horizon states its score agrees with the student and provides no corrective signal. In our experiments, neither choice improved over the base model across broad range of configurations. Per-sample teacher-free objectives avoid the teacher altogether, yet they supervise only states that can be paired with ground truth, where degraded history is followed by its recorded continuation or reversed rollout recovers its recorded start. Because the regression target always lies within recorded clip, the states rollout visits thousands of frames later are never supervised directly. We take different view of the supervision required for long rollouts. Rather than matching teacher or paired recording, rollout must remain realistic continuation of its own scene while following the commanded actions. These properties call for complementary training signals. Visual realism and scene fidelity are distributional properties of the rollout that can be evaluated against real data at any horizon, because the distribution of real frames is fixed, compact to summarise and, unlike learned score, equally valid one second or one minute into rollout. Action following, by contrast, is property of conditional generation that the "

Text:"Learning to Discriminate Scene Structures Makes Self-Supervised Depth Learning ScalableZizhan Guo Wei Ye Rui Fan(cid:66)Figure 1: Beyond (a) achieving SoTA performance across diverse datasets with unified model, MaSS is the first SSMDE method to (b) effectively benefit from increased data diversity and (c) exhibit remarkable generalizability to entirely out-of-distribution scenes.Self-supervised monocular depth estimation is appealing for its potential to scale with unlabeled data, yet existing methods often degrade when trained on diverse scenes. This study finds that, under self-supervision, models tend to encode datasetspecific structural biases rather than transferable scene geometry, leading to crossdomain optimization conflicts. To address this issue, this study proposes the MaSS framework, aiming to make self-supervised depth learning scalable to diverse data. MaSS introduces dedicated structural representation pathway explicitly decoupled from contextual features, which is guided by novel scene-structure discrimination objective to learn generalized, layout-aware structural representations for depth decoding. Trained and evaluated on four diverse datasets, both individually and jointly, MaSS substantially outperforms prior self-supervised methods and is the first to consistently benefit from mixed-domain training. Zero-shot evaluation on eight additional datasets further demonstrates its remarkable generalizability, particularly on those entirely out-of-distribution scenes. The source code is publicly available at https://mias.group/MaSS. (cid:66) Corresponding author. 40th Conference on Neural Information Processing Systems (NeurIPS 2026).The fundamental advantage of self-supervised learning lies in its potential to leverage ubiquitous unlabeled data for scalable model training. However, most existing self-supervised monocular depth estimation (SSMDE) research [1, 2, 3, 4, 5] is predominantly confined to training models individually on single datasets or scenarios, leaving much of this potential unexplored. Recent efforts have attempted to directly train models with existing SSMDE methods on large-scale video sequences curated from online platforms [6, 7], as well as to incorporate vision foundation models (VFMs) as backbones [8, 7]. Despite these advances, overall performance improvements remain limited. As demonstrated by our preliminary experiments (see Fig. 1(b)), when existing methods are trained simultaneously on diverse monocular video data (e.g., indoor, outdoor, dynamic, and adverse weather scenarios), they fail to benefit mutually from the increased diversity and do not exhibit improved generalization to out-of-distribution scenes. Instead, we observe performance decline compared to models trained individually on single dataset. This observation demonstrates that existing SSMDE methods have not yet achieved effective scalability to diverse data. This study begins by investigating the challenges that arise when SSMDE is exposed to diverse data distributions during training. It is worth noting that the novel-view synthesis-based supervision inevitably introduces noisy and artifact-laden supervisory signals in scenarios involving occlusions, textureless regions, and illumination changes [9, 10]. Even setting aside such noise, the gradients induced by self-supervised losses often fail to reflect depth errors faithfully [4]. As result, the optimization process is inherently prone to local minima, leading to poor convergence stability [4]. Although these challenges frequently occur during self-supervised training, existing methods can still achieve reasonable accuracy on single, homogeneous dataset [3]. This is largely because the network can exploit the structural bias inherent to that dataset, thereby compensating for weak supervision. The key evidence we find is that under the existing SSMDE framework, the coarsest, high-level feature of the depth encoder does not capture depth-related cues, such as the foregroundbackground relation and perspective geometry. Instead, it primarily reflects the dataset-specific structural biases. When training on diverse datasets, different data domains often require the model to learn inconsistent structural biases for their respective convergence, leading to the observed performance drop. These findings motivate us to explicitly guide the network to learn robust, generalized scene-structure representation for depth decoding. Based on this core idea, we develop the MaSS framework that Make Self-supervised depth learning Scalable to diverse data. MaSS bridges conventional depth encoder with frozen pretrained VFM through bidirectional interaction architecture, drawing inspiration from [11]. In this design, the depth encoder focuses on extracting geometric cues that are difficult to recover from VFM features [8]. The interacted VFM features are then fed into two feature projectors to reconstruct complementary, collapse-free representations under explicit constraints and learning objectives. Specifically, one projector is adapted to learn contextual representation informative for depth estimation, such as object-level spatial relationships, while retaining generalized semantics with the original VFM features as anchors. The other projector is then constrained to be decoupled from the contextual one, thereby reserving its representational capacity for scene structure. To explicitly define this representation, we introduce scene-structure discrimination objective via contrastive learning. This objective organizes the feature space based on scene layout similarity, which is measured by the histogram distribution of predicted depth. Driven by this objective, the second projector learns transferable structural representations that capture global scene layout and perspective geometry, rather than dataset-specific biases. Together, these two complementary representations enable more reliable depth predictions while reducing their reliance on dataset-specific biases. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MaSS outperforms previous state-of-the-art (SoTA) selfsupervised methods by large margin (as presented in Fig. 1(a)) and achieves accuracy comparable to certain supervised depth foundation models. Moreover, MaSS represents the first self-supervised approach to achieve clear performance gains from increasing the diversity of training data (Fig. 1(b)), resulting in notable generalizability improvements particularly on out-of-distribution scen"

Text:"Vision-Language-Delta-Action: Learning Compact Deltas as Actionable Transition Interfaces for Robotic Manipulation Lingsen Zhang1,2, Wei Li1,2, Jie He1,2 Henghua Zhang2 Kai Mo2 Shiyong Li2 Yanpeng Wang2 Rui Shao1,3, Equal Contribution Corresponding Author 1Harbin Institute of Technology, Shenzhen 2Baidu, Inc. 3Shenzhen Loop Area Institute Robotic manipulation is not merely observation-to-action generation, but the realization of task-specified state changes in the physical world. Existing vision-language-action (VLA) methods directly learn observation-toaction mappings, imposing substantial learning burden on policies due to the large gap between semantic understanding and action execution. Although world-action models (WAMs) improve control through future prediction, they optimize the full future rather than task-relevant changes, wasting capacity on static appearance or action-irrelevant abstractions. To address this issue, we propose Vision-Language-Delta-Action (VLDA), new paradigm that reformulates full future prediction as compact actionable transition modeling. Our core insight is to introduce compact Delta as an intermediate decision variable that captures latent world-state changes, serving as unified representational interface between vision-language understanding and action generation. To learn such Delta representation, VLDA introduces unified objective composed of the Local Transition Filter (LT-Filter) and Global Transition Anchor (GT-Anchor). LT-Filter captures taskrelevant manipulation-centric changes, preventing Delta from being biased toward static patch reconstruction. GT-Anchor provides coarse future-level regularization, ensuring that Delta can explain the overall trend of future changes. Building on these, Delta-Driven Action Refinement (DDAR) further grounds Delta in the action manifold and injects it as structured seed to guide action denoising. We implement VLDA within LoongForge, an open-source training framework for large-scale foundation "

Text:"Factorization-Guided Training and Inference for Diffusion-Drafted Speculative Decoding Jiakun Fan1, 3, Min Xiao2, 3, Qiang Wang3, Neiwen Ling3, Zhichao Liu3, Zhihao Jia2, Dimitrios S. Nikolopoulos1, Zijun Wei3 1Virginia Tech, 2Carnegie Mellon University, 3ByteDance Equal contribution, Corresponding author Speculative decoding accelerates large language model inference through parallel verification of tokens proposed by drafter. Diffusion drafters further parallelize proposal generation, but greater proposal capacity or higher mean accepted tokens (MAT) need not yield higher throughput. Because verification accepts only consecutive prefix, early rejections can render later correct predictions ineffective, limiting MAT gains, while even higher MAT may not improve throughput if per-round latency increases. We introduce DScout, which uses two complementary factorizations to guide diffusion drafter training and inference for higher throughput. For training, DScout factorizes position-wise loss into inclusion, weighting, and prediction loss. This guides the design and combination of mixedhorizon training, intra-block unmasking, and acceptance-weighted loss to align supervision with prefix acceptance and improve MAT. For inference, DScout factorizes throughput as the ratio of MAT to per-round latency, guiding target-conditioning depth, drafter depth, and inference block size selection to identify high-throughput configuration for each workload. Across six benchmarks, DScout achieves 3.84 and 4.14 mean speedups over autoregressive decoding on Qwen3-4B and Qwen3-8B, respectively, improving mean speedup over the state-of-the-art baseline by 9.1% and 7.1%. Across models and concurrency settings, our inference configuration policy provides up to 9.75% additional mean speedup over DScouts fixed configuration. Text:"HandsOn 2026-10-13 HandsOn: Large-Scale Simulation Benchmark for Generalizable Dexterous Manipulation Tianwei Luo1,3,,, Yiming He1,,, Hongjin Wu1,, Ting Pan2,, Debin Ren3,, Haorui Guo3, Junlin Yang1, Yuxin Zuo1, Zhehao Kou1, Kaisen Yang1, Zhuoqun Lai3, Yi Ding3 Jiale Yu1, Yuchen Zhang4, Zijie Qiu1, Haozhan Li1,3, Ning Ding1,3, 1Tsinghua University 3NatureWill # luotw23@mails.tsinghua.edu.cn, dingning@tsinghua.edu.cn Code: https://github.com/TsinghuaC3I/HandsOn 2Beijing University of Posts and Telecommunications 4Peking University Equal contribution. Project lead. Corresponding author. Abstract General-purpose manipulation requires reliable behavior across embodiments, objects, and task demands. We introduce HandsOn, composable simulation platform and benchmark for cross-embodiment dexterous manipulation. It supports five robotic arms, six dexterous hands, and approximately 200 rigid, articulated, and deformable assets in randomized scenes. The benchmark pairs 18,000 curated human demonstrations with 66 human-verified tasks: Core-50 supports demonstration-based learning and execution tests, while Transfer-16 evaluates asset and relation transfer, skill composition, and deformable manipulation without target-task training demonstrations. Qualitative real-robot rollouts illustrate sim-to-real transfer. Across diffusion, vision-language-action, world-action, and prompted VLM-based methods, the best trained multi-task policy achieves 29.1% Core-50 success. Without benchmark finetuning, GPT-6 using move_eef for palm-and-finger control achieves 5.2% on Core-50 and 1.9% on Transfer-16 over 660 trials. Trained-policy stage diagnostics expose failures in contact establishment, action coordination, and maintaining achieved goals, with additional sequencing errors in composed tasks. HandsOn provides shared basis for investigating manipulation generalization and the limits of reliable physical interaction. Figure 1 Overview of HandsOn. Representative assets, environments and int"

We extend this paradigm to recommendation for decision-making, focusing on decisions where the set of possible choices is fixed but the reasons for choosing them are not. In high-stakes decisions, e.g., which company to acquire or which customer to target, comparing diverse, well-reasoned options can be more useful than single ranked list. We formalize openended recommendation as building diverse, high-quality set of recommendations, each an item together with hypothesis, natural-language reason why the item fits the needs of decision maker. We propose QD-Rec, qualitydiversity method that selects items with contextual bandit and generates hypotheses with foundation model from earlier ones kept in an archive as stepping stones. new recommendation enters the archive if it is novel or better than its nearest neighbor. Across M&A, customer targeting, and paper and book recommendation, QD-Rec gives the highest mean quality and builds the largest archive in most settings. Code: github.com/SakanaAI/QD-Rec 1. Abstract Robots share execution experience across fleets to improve policies via failure-guided retrieval and asynchronous training, achieving high success without extra expert corrections.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Experience generated during deployment offers an opportunity for robots to improve policy reliability through learning from their own executions. However, learning through human corrections requires teleoperators to monitor execution and intervene when policies fail, a demand that is difficult to meet as robot fleets grow. We introduce collective experience learning (CEL), a learning paradigm in which deployed robots share their execution experience to improve a common policy. We realize CEL through failure-guided experience retrieval fine-tuning (FERT), which uses failure contexts from robot executions to retrieve relevant trajectory segments from the fleet's shared successful experience and uses their actions as supervision for policy updates, without collecting additional expert demonstrations or corrective actions. A fault-tolerant asynchronous training system (FATS) coordinates experience collection, policy optimization and policy distribution despite local interruptions within the robot fleet. Across five real-world manipulation tasks, the resulting policies achieve success rates of 93.3-99.2%, evaluated over 120 trials per task on robots excluded from training data collection. With collection time per robot held fixed, larger fleets provide more experience and FERT generally achieves higher success rates across the evaluated sizes. On multi-stage phone packaging, learning with 96 robots increases success from 79.2% to 98.3%. These results support collective experience as a practical basis for scalable robot learning and a path towards improving policy reliability during deployment without collecting additional expert corrective actions.
[20.10.2026 05:39] Response: {"desc": "В статье предлагается коллективное обучение на опыте (collective experience learning, CEL), при котором развернутые роботы делятся данными о своих выполнениях для улучшения общей политики (policy). Метод FERT использует контекст сбоев, чтобы через retrieval находить релевантные успешные фрагменты траекторий из общего опыта флота и применять их действия как supervision для fine-tuning, без сбора дополнительных экспертных демонстраций. Для координации сбора данных, оптимизации политики и её распространения применяется отказоустойчивая асинхронная система обучения FATS. В пяти реальных задачах манипуляции успешность политик достигает 93.3–99.2%, а на задаче упаковки телефонов с 96 роботами успешность выросла с 79.2% до 98.3%.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Роботы учатся друг у друга на своих ошибках"}

Abstract OpenVLHarness improves multimodal reasoning by integrating persistent memory, heterogeneous tool interfaces, and iterative evidence composition across 22 benchmarks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have made rapid progress on visual reasoning, yet many tasks require evidence that is difficult to obtain through internal reasoning alone: our controlled study on 22 benchmarks shows that simply increasing the inference budget of the base model yields only modest gains. Such tasks demand grounded perception, external knowledge, and numerical measurements obtained through heterogeneous tools, whose outputs must be made interpretable to the model and retained across reasoning steps. We introduce OpenVLHarness, a multimodal reasoning harness organized around a capability layer and persistent multimodal memory. The capability layer combines task-level tool interfaces with evidence rendering, providing consistent access to heterogeneous backends and converting their outputs into model-readable observations. Persistent multimodal memory retains the corresponding images, structured data, and web references as addressable artifacts throughout each query. A coding interface executes generated programs over stored data, enabling computations that combine results from multiple tools. Together, these mechanisms support an iterative process of acquiring, inspecting, and composing multimodal evidence. Evaluated on 22 benchmarks with eight open and proprietary backbones, OpenVLHarness improves average scores by 9.7-12.6 points over matched no-tool baselines, including +11.2 with Qwen3-VL-8B, +9.7 with Kimi K3, and +9.9 with GPT-6 Astra. With GPT-6 Luna at medium reasoning effort, OpenVLHarness outperforms the base model, Codex, and Visual Sketchpad at maximum effort by 7.7-8.5 points while incurring at most 37% of their model-token cost.

Abstract MT Forcing improves multi-turn image editing by combining single-turn distribution matching distillation and teacher-forcing guidance to stabilize visual quality and preserve cross-turn consistency.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Multi-turn image editing aims to modify an image according to a sequence of instructions, enabling more effective interactive visual content creation for users. However, existing methods often exhibit noticeable drift after only a few editing turns, manifesting in two aspects: 1) unstable visual quality, with accumulating artifacts and degraded details; and 2) inconsistent image content, with unintended changes to preserved regions and prior edits. To address these challenges, we propose MT Forcing, a novel training paradigm for multi-turn image editing with unified multimodal models (UMMs) based on autoregressive diffusion. Specifically, we propose Single-Turn Distribution Matching Distillation (Single-Turn DMD), which supervises student self-rollouts with a teacher that applies cumulative edits to the source image in a single turn, providing visual-quality guidance without recursive error accumulation. Additionally, we introduce Teacher-Forcing Guidance, which supervises history-dependent transitions on reference trajectories during warmup and as a regularizer during distillation, encouraging cross-turn content consistency beyond what per-turn distribution matching alone can ensure. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our approach significantly improves the multi-turn image editing capabilities of UMMs, yielding more stable visual quality and stronger cross-turn consistency.

Авторы предлагают Single-Turn DMD, где ученическая модель обучается на собственных самогенерациях с учителем, который применяет все накопленные правки за один ход, что исключает рекурсивное накопление ошибок. Дополнительно вводится Teacher-Forcing Guidance, который на этапе прогрева и как регуляризатор во время дистилляции направляет зависимые от истории переходы по эталонным траекториям для сохранения согласованности содержимого между ходами. Abstract Self-HPO is a recursive framework that alternates harness and policy optimization for coding agents, improving performance by targeting evolving failure modes through reinforcement learning.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Large language model (LLM) agents have shown promising potential in coding tasks, and their performance is jointly determined by the policy model and the harness that mediates their interaction with the environment. Indeed, harness and policy are mutually reinforcing and evolving only one component is suboptimal---a harness tuned for one checkpoint may not target the failure modes of an updated policy, while policy learning remains limited to the trajectories elicited by a fixed harness. In light of this, we propose Self-HPO, a recursive self-improving framework for coding agents that alternates harness and policy optimization, using the evolving policy as its own harness proposer. Specifically, in each round, the current policy first analyzes its own failures and proposes new harness candidates (H-step). The policy is then updated through reinforcement learning (RL) under the selected harness (P-step), and hard tasks identified during training form the next harness search set, keeping harness optimization targeted to the policy's evolving failure modes. Across three coding benchmarks, Self-HPO consistently outperforms harness-only and policy-only baselines, improving Qwen3.5-35B-A3B's average resolve rate from 57.8% to 64.8%, with gains generalizing to out-of-distribution settings. Further analysis shows that both optimized components retain gains even when paired with their base counterparts, while their joint gain exceeds the sum of their individual gains. Self-HPO also improves reliability (Pass@1) and expands the capability frontier (Pass@k) over the base agent. Additionally, we show that with a stronger external model (e.g., Opus 4.8) as the harness proposer, the same framework yields a further performance boost.

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract Self-HPO is a recursive framework that alternates harness and policy optimization for coding agents, improving performance by targeting evolving failure modes through reinforcement learning.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Large language model (LLM) agents have shown promising potential in coding tasks, and their performance is jointly determined by the policy model and the harness that mediates their interaction with the environment. Indeed, harness and policy are mutually reinforcing and evolving only one component is suboptimal---a harness tuned for one checkpoint may not target the failure modes of an updated policy, while policy learning remains limited to the trajectories elicited by a fixed harness. In light of this, we propose Self-HPO, a recursive self-improving framework for coding agents that alternates harness and policy optimization, using the evolving policy as its own harness proposer. Specifically, in each round, the current policy first analyzes its own failures and proposes new harness candidates (H-step). The policy is then updated through reinforcement learning (RL) under the selected harness (P-step), and hard tasks identified during training form the next harness search set, keeping harness optimization targeted to the policy's evolving failure modes. Across three coding benchmarks, Self-HPO consistently outperforms harness-only and policy-only baselines, improving Qwen3.5-35B-A3B's average resolve rate from 57.8% to 64.8%, with gains generalizing to out-of-distribution settings. Further analysis shows that both optimized components retain gains even when paired with their base counterparts, while their joint gain exceeds the sum of their individual gains. Self-HPO also improves reliability (Pass@1) and expands the capability frontier (Pass@k) over the base agent. Additionally, we show that with a stronger external model (e.g., Opus 4.8) as the harness proposer, the same framework yields a further performance boost."

Abstract RoAR-VAE improves visual tokenizers by enforcing robust prediction of semantics and reconstruction from corrupted latents, balancing fidelity with diffusion learnability.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small An effective visual tokenizer must preserve image fidelity while producing latents that downstream diffusion models can learn efficiently. Prior work improves latent diffusability through semantic alignment or joint tokenizer--diffusion training. Revisiting REPA-E, a representative approach, we find that removing its diffusion objective and dedicated generative blocks while retaining semantic prediction from corrupted latents yields comparable downstream generation performance under a matched setting. This observation motivates a general principle of robust prediction: generation-relevant information should remain recoverable from corrupted latents. We instantiate this principle with two complementary objectives. RobustAlign predicts pretrained semantic features from corrupted latents to preserve high-level structure, while RobustRecon reconstructs the clean image to retain fine-grained spatial and appearance information. Together, they form Robust Alignment and Reconstruction VAE (RoAR-VAE). Our analysis suggests that robust prediction improves diffusability by making useful information more accessible in high-variance latent directions, which diffusion models tend to learn earlier. Empirically, RoAR-VAE improves average generation benchmark performance by 2.19 points over the strongest baseline tokenizer under the same training budget, while improving small-text reconstruction accuracy by 16.93 percentage points over FLUX.1-VAE. These results demonstrate that RoAR-VAE achieves a favorable balance between reconstruction fidelity and downstream generative learnability.

Авторы показывают, что генерационно-значимая информация должна оставаться восстановимой даже из искажённых латентов, что формулируется как принцип robust prediction. Этот принцип реализован через два дополнительных целевых функционала: RobustAlign, предсказывающий семантические признаки предобученной модели из искажённых латентов, и RobustRecon, восстанавливающий чистое изображение. Abstract CoworkEnv synthesizes executable training environments for AI coworkers using workflow-guided task construction and programmatic validation, enabling supervised fine-tuning and reinforcement learning of compact PocketWorker models that improve multi-turn coordination and tool use.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small AI coworkers must coordinate tools, communicate across turns, and act on changing workspace state while respecting workflow constraints. Constructing training experience for these capabilities requires coherent requests, service records, tool interfaces, and grading checks. We present CoworkEnv, a capability-guided framework for synthesizing executable training environments for AI coworkers. Workflow requirements guide task construction, and programmatic validation with feedback repair checks generated specifications and grading behavior. Teacher executions yield curated demonstrations for supervised fine-tuning (SFT), while student interactions provide rollouts and rewards for reinforcement learning (RL). Using these resources, we train compact PocketWorker models. For Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct, SFT raises the mean score from 46.6 to 64.1 on Claw-Eval, including a 31.9-point gain on Multi-turn tasks. An SFT-only extension to ClawMark demonstrates applicability across coworker benchmarks. We further compare RL training dynamics across configurations that vary reward processing and credit assignment. Improving training rewards provide preliminary evidence that the constructed environments support policy optimization.

Сгенерированные среды используются для supervised fine-tuning (SFT) на демонстрациях учителя и для reinforcement learning (RL) по наградам, получаемым в ходе взаимодействия студента со средой. Workflow requirements guide task construction, and programmatic validation with feedback repair checks generated specifications and grading behavior. Teacher executions yield curated demonstrations for supervised fine-tuning (SFT), while student interactions provide rollouts and rewards for reinforcement learning (RL). Using these resources, we train compact PocketWorker models. For Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct, SFT raises the mean score from 46.6 to 64.1 on Claw-Eval, including a 31.9-point gain on Multi-turn tasks. An SFT-only extension to ClawMark demonstrates applicability across coworker benchmarks. We further compare RL training dynamics across configurations that vary reward processing and credit assignment. Improving training rewards provide preliminary evidence that the constructed environments support policy optimization."

Abstract OR-License evaluates whether LLM agents can solve real-world operations research problems from original client materials, finding that while some achieve high objective quality, none match expert-level feasibility and deployment-adjusted quality.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Can large language model (LLM) agents deliver expert-level operations research (OR) solutions directly from original client materials, without prior expert reformulation? Existing OR benchmarks typically begin with self-contained descriptions or predefined optimization tasks, underrepresenting the translation of original business materials into professionally validated solutions. We introduce OR-License, an end-to-end benchmark of 31 instances across 10 industrial scenarios from paid OR engagements totaling USD 3.68 million. It preserves original requirements, input data, deliverable formats, and information boundaries after de-identification. To study information acquisition, we introduce ORSim, a controlled client simulator that discloses business facts without supplying OR expertise. Hidden evaluation checks feasibility and compares objective quality against original expert solutions accepted by clients before benchmark construction. Deployment-Adjusted Quality (DAQ) averages expert-relative quality across instances, assigning zero to infeasible deliveries. Across 11 frontier LLMs using coding-agent harnesses, several exceed the expert reference in conditional objective quality, but none matches its DAQ; the strongest primary Claude Code configuration achieves 88.39% strict feasibility and a DAQ of 0.87 against the expert reference of 1. Client information acquisition extends beyond clarification into solution development and validation, with some later stages showing higher confirmed issue-resolution proportions. Trajectory analysis identifies formulation and implementation failures, while task-equalized research-chain completion rates range from 24% to 45% across models on evaluable tasks. These findings motivate sustained information-gap resolution, faithful problem formulation, and evidence-linked self-improvement for reliable long-horizon agents.

Они также создали ORSim, симулятор клиента, который раскрывает бизнес-факты без экспертных знаний OR, чтобы проверять, как LLM-агенты собирают информацию. Метрика DAQ оценивает качество решений относительно экспертных, присваивая ноль недопустимым решениям. Результаты показывают, что ни одна из 11 современных LLM не достигает экспертного уровня по DAQ, хотя некоторые превосходят эталон по целевой функции, и выявляют ошибки формализации и реализации.", "emoji": "📊", "title": "LLM-агенты пока не заменят эксперта в исследовании операций: даже лучшие решения уступают людям по реализуемости"}

Abstract ScaleNav learns goal-directed visual dynamics from internet egocentric videos to generate robot actions for language-conditioned local navigation, achieving high success rates in simulation and real-world deployment.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small We study language-conditioned local navigation, where a robot must navigate to a text-specified target visible in its initial observation and stop at the described location. End-to-end navigation policies require diverse training data to generalize across scenes, yet real-robot data is costly to collect and simulated data leaves a sim-to-real gap. Internet egocentric videos offer a scalable source of real-world navigation experience. Our key insight is to learn goal-directed visual dynamics from Internet egocentric videos, and use these dynamics to support robot action generation. To this end, we propose ScaleNav, a unified world-action framework with three-stage training. We first perform instruction-conditioned video pretraining on these videos to learn how observations evolve as the camera approaches instructed targets across diverse real-world scenes. Action alignment then uses action-labeled simulated data to train the model to predict executable action chunks. Joint fine-tuning subsequently optimizes all trainable components together for navigation. ScaleNav achieves success rates of 86.5% on a single-view adaptation of Short-Horizon OVON and 94.9% on our HabitatGS-LN benchmark, outperforming the evaluated baselines on both benchmarks. We further deploy and evaluate ScaleNav on a real quadruped robot, with results supporting the model's ability to generalize to real-world navigation. Code and videos are available at https://zju3dv.github.io/ScaleNav/
[20.10.2026 05:40] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен ScaleNav — унифицированная world-action модель для языково-обусловленной локальной навигации робота, где цель задаётся текстом. Ключевая идея: обучать goal-directed visual dynamics на интернет-видео от первого лица, чтобы модель понимала, как наблюдения меняются при приближении к целевому объекту. Обучение проходит в три этапа: instruction-conditioned video pretraining, action alignment на симуляционных данных с разметкой действий и совместный fine-tuning всех обучаемых компонентов. Модель достигает успешности 86.5% и 94.9% на бенчмарках и демонстрирует работоспособность на реальном четырёхногом роботе.", "emoji": "🧭", "title": "Учим робота ориентироваться по видео из интернета"}

Abstract Researchers introduce AnyGoal to evaluate whether world models can generalize dynamics to unseen combinations of familiar states and actions for planning.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small The goal of a world model is to capture the dynamics of an environment. This has the great appeal that it allows agents to plan and simulate a course of actions with the help of a world model. For these uses to be reliable, however, the learned dynamics must behave well beyond the specific state-action combinations observed in expert rollouts. Despite this, the relationship between data coverage, dynamics generalization, and downstream planning performance remains underexplored. We study this relationship through the lens of compositional generalization: can a world model recombine familiar state and action factors into valid dynamics? To answer this, we introduce AnyGoal, a benchmark of goal-shifted task variants built from widely used world-model environments, together with an open-source library. Each task is designed to be within reach of a world model that has learned the underlying dynamics: individual state factors and actions remain within their training support, but their combinations are underrepresented in expert trajectories. Across multiple environments and world model families, we find that current models consistently exhibit failures under these recombinations. By examining dynamics and representation failures across model families, interaction regimes, and evaluation metrics, we study where current world models generalize and where challenges remain for goal-shifted prediction and planning. Together, this framework, benchmark, and analysis provide a basis for evaluating progress toward world models that generalize to new planning tasks.

Тестовые задачи построены так, что отдельные факторы состояния и действия находятся в пределах обучающей выборки, но их сочетания редко встречаются в экспертных траекториях. Эксперименты на нескольких средах и семействах моделей показывают, что текущие модели стабильно ошибаются при таких рекомбинациях. Анализ ошибок динамики и представлений помогает понять, где модели хорошо обобщают, а где для планирования с новыми целями остаются serious проблемы.", "emoji": "🧭", "title": "Мир предсказуем лишь там, где модель видела его раньше: проверка обобщения world models"}

Abstract InfiniDream is a vision-language model framework that generates open-ended, editable, photorealistic 3D worlds through symbolic procedural asset creation and deterministic scene placement.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Procedural generation plays a key role in synthetic data creation and game development, offering greater controllability, editability, and diversity than static mesh representations. However, VLM-based code generation often produces relatively simple 3D assets, while sophisticated photorealistic procedural systems require extensive manual development. Existing VLM-guided procedural methods remain largely constrained by predefined factories and APIs, limiting their ability to create novel asset types. We introduce InfiniDream, a VLM-driven framework for open-ended procedural 3D world generation. First, we propose Symbolically Constrained Asset Generation, which leverages existing procedural algorithms to create novel, photorealistic assets while preserving editability and seed-based diversity. Second, we develop an end-to-end text-to-world pipeline that combines semantic scene planning with Deterministic Frustum-Guided Grid Placement to construct large-scale 3D worlds with precise object placement, procedural population, and animation. Extensive evaluations on 100 object and 20 scene generation tasks demonstrate improvements across four perceptual metrics. Diversity analysis, ablation studies, and human preference evaluations further validate the effectiveness of our framework. Project page: https://yichuanh.github.io/infinidream-page/

Ключевая идея Symbolically Constrained Asset Generation: VLM пишет символьные описания, а существующие процедурные алгоритмы создают новые ассеты без ограничения заранее заданным набором API, сохраняя редактируемость и разнообразие через seed. Для сборки целых сцен используется семантическое планирование и Deterministic Frustum-Guided Grid Placement, обеспечивающий точное размещение объектов, процедурное заполнение и анимацию. Abstract Parameter-efficient fine-tuning with low-rank adapters enables efficient adaptation of very large multimodal backbones for brain encoding, improving performance and interpretability while reducing computational cost.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Brain encoding models aim to predict neural responses to stimuli. The most widely used approach fits readouts from frozen pretrained backbones. Using neural data to train large models from scratch or fully fine-tune them after task-pretraining has proven difficult because neural data is costly and limited. We show that parameter-efficient fine-tuning (PEFT) enables us to effectively adapt very large pretrained backbones for brain encoding with a small number of trainable parameters. To make it computationally practical, we exploit the backbone's causal structure to predict multiple timepoints in a single pass instead of using sliding windows, significantly reducing feature-extraction cost. This enables efficient fine-tuning of multimodal backbones with up to 104B parameters, the largest model adapted for brain encoding to our knowledge. Trained with a low-rank adapter (LoRA) and a lightweight readout, our approach consistently improves brain encoding performance across nine diverse multimodal backbones. On the same Qwen3-Omni backbone as the prior state-of-the-art (SoTA), our method BrainLoRA achieves the best published performance on the Algonauts 2025 Challenge. We also show the adapted multimodal backbones remain interpretable: modality attribution recovers the canonical sensory topography that prior work often obtains by construction from separate unimodal encoders, and neural adaptation increases the contribution of audio inputs and cross-modal mixing within the backbone. To scale out the number of adapted models, we develop a multi-LoRA kernel that accelerates joint training of multiple LoRAs on a shared backbone. This enables an ensemble of 200+ adapted models that ranks first on the public Algonauts post-challenge leaderboard at the time of writing. Together, our results highlight PEFT as an efficient and powerful paradigm for brain encoding.

"Abstract Parameter-efficient fine-tuning with low-rank adapters enables efficient adaptation of very large multimodal backbones for brain encoding, improving performance and interpretability while reducing computational cost.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Brain encoding models aim to predict neural responses to stimuli. The most widely used approach fits readouts from frozen pretrained backbones. To scale out the number of adapted models, we develop a multi-LoRA kernel that accelerates joint training of multiple LoRAs on a shared backbone. This enables an ensemble of 200+ adapted models that ranks first on the public Algonauts post-challenge leaderboard at the time of writing. Together, our results highlight PEFT as an efficient and powerful paradigm for brain encoding."

Abstract EgoXperience introduces a unified framework for joint reconstruction of camera motion, scene geometry, hands, and objects from egocentric video using automatically annotated large-scale data and a single-pass model.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Reconstructing human activity from egocentric video requires more than recovering individual 3D components: the observer's motion, surrounding geometry, and hand-object interactions must fit together in a consistent world. Achieving this coherence requires both joint reconstruction and aligned supervision, yet existing methods and datasets offer limited support for either. We present EgoXperience, a full-stack paradigm that addresses these needs through data collection, automatic annotation, unified modeling, and benchmarking. Central to EgoXperience is an automatic annotation engine, EgoX-Engine, that couples geometrically verified object tracking with SLAM-based world alignment, yielding consistent supervision for the camera, scene, hands, and objects. This yields EgoX-Data-6M, a 50-hour dataset of task-driven manipulation and locomotion within and across indoor and outdoor environments, with second-level task captions. We carefully select one hour across these activity categories as EgoX-Bench to evaluate joint reconstruction. To translate this supervision into reconstruction, we introduce EgoX, trained with EgoX-Data-6M and complementary annotations from additional public datasets. Given monocular egocentric video and first-frame object masks, our model achieves joint metric reconstruction of the camera, scene, hands, and objects in a shared world coordinate frame with a single forward pass. Extensive experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art performance across scene estimation and world-space hand-object pose recovery. Against the strongest evaluated baselines, EgoX reduces camera, hand, and depth errors by 68.9%, 65.6%, and 42.9%, and improves object pose scores by 28.0%.

Ключевой компонент — автоматический движок аннотации EgoX-Engine, который сочетает геометрически верифицированное трекинг объектов с SLAM-выравниванием мира и создаёт датасет EgoX-Data-6M объёмом 50 часов. Abstract HOLI-WORLD is an integrated ecosystem that automates the construction of large-scale learning resources from egocentric recordings to improve machine understanding of dynamic scenes and interactions.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Human egocentric experience offers a rich foundation for learning how the world is organized and how it changes through interaction. Realizing this potential at scale requires turning growing volumes of recordings into learning resources that connect scene structure, object semantics, and interaction histories across viewpoints and time, while reducing dependence on costly manual annotation. We introduce HOLI-WORLD, an integrated ecosystem connecting experience capture, world representation, automated data construction, model learning, and independent evaluation. To sup- port learning at scale, HOLIWORLD-Foundry combines complementary static and dynamic perception to automatically construct world records that connect persistent scene structure and evolving object states with interaction histories and supporting observations. HOLIWORLD-Data organizes these records from approximately 100 self-collected scenes into grounded learning resources, including approximately 80K QA pairs used to train HOLIWORLD-VL-4B. Independent human annotations of heldout recordings form HOLIWORLD-Bench, supporting evaluation of both annotation quality and learned spatial and temporal understanding. Experiments on ScanNet, ScanNet++, Aria Digital Twin, and HOLIWORLD-Bench demonstrate strong performance in static perception and dynamic tracking, while training on HOLIWORLD-Data substantially improves HOLIWORLD-VL-4B's spatial and temporal reasoning on HOLI- WORLD-Bench and EgoDynamic4D. HOLIWORLD provides a practical foundation for expanding learning resources from the continuous flow of human experience and advancing machine understanding of the dynamic world.
[20.10.2026 05:41] Response: {"desc": "Авторы представляют HOLI-WORLD — экосистему, которая автоматически создаёт большие обучающие наборы данных из записей от первого лица (egocentric recordings) о динамических сценах и взаимодействиях. Система HOLIWORLD-Foundry объединяет статическое и динамическое восприятие, чтобы строить структурированные записи о мире: устойчивую геометрию сцены, состояния объектов и историю взаимодействий. Paper text to classify:

"Abstract HOLI-WORLD is an integrated ecosystem that automates the construction of large-scale learning resources from egocentric recordings to improve machine understanding of dynamic scenes and interactions.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Human egocentric experience offers a rich foundation for learning how the world is organized and how it changes through interaction. Realizing this potential at scale requires turning growing volumes of recordings into learning resources that connect scene structure, object semantics, and interaction histories across viewpoints and time, while reducing dependence on costly manual annotation. We introduce HOLI-WORLD, an integrated ecosystem connecting experience capture, world representation, automated data construction, model learning, and independent evaluation. To sup- port learning at scale, HOLIWORLD-Foundry combines complementary static and dynamic perception to automatically construct world records that connect persistent scene structure and evolving object states with interaction histories and supporting observations. HOLIWORLD-Data organizes these records from approximately 100 self-collected scenes into grounded learning resources, including approximately 80K QA pairs used to train HOLIWORLD-VL-4B. Independent human annotations of heldout recordings form HOLIWORLD-Bench, supporting evaluation of both annotation quality and learned spatial and temporal understanding. Experiments on ScanNet, ScanNet++, Aria Digital Twin, and HOLIWORLD-Bench demonstrate strong performance in static perception and dynamic tracking, while training on HOLIWORLD-Data substantially improves HOLIWORLD-VL-4B's spatial and temporal reasoning on HOLI- WORLD-Bench and EgoDynamic4D. HOLIWORLD provides a practical foundation for expanding learning resources from the continuous flow of human experience and advancing machine understanding of the dynamic world."

Abstract HarnessPro evolves AI agent harnesses without labels to boost task success and lower execution costs, yielding lightweight, balanced, and high-performance variants via trajectory diagnosis and weighted selection.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Harness development plays a crucial role in building capable AI agents. Recent approaches improve harnesses through recursive self-improvement (RSI), but their performance gains often come with higher execution costs. We introduce HarnessPro, a label-free method for evolving harnesses to improve task performance while reducing execution cost. In this setting, candidate harnesses are evaluated using LLM-generated tests without access to ground-truth solutions or official tests. Specialized agents diagnose execution trajectories, plan updates with different performance-cost priorities, and implement them as candidate harnesses. Rather than returning a single harness, HarnessPro uses normalized weighted squared-distance selection to provide cost-focused (lite), balanced (medium), and performance-focused (heavy) harnesses for different deployment budgets. Selected harnesses enter the next iteration, where execution feedback guides further updates. Our experiments cover Terminal-Bench 2, SWE-Bench Pro, and ProgramBench, using GPT-5.5 with Codex and GLM-5.2 with Claude Code. Across these benchmarks using GPT-5.5 with Codex, the heavy mode of HarnessPro achieves an average pass-rate gain of 2.0 percentage points and an average per-task cost reduction of 26.1% relative to RHO. This advantage also holds when using GLM-5.2 with Claude Code. Code is available at https://github.com/pzs19/HarnessPro

Кандидатные обвязки оцениваются на тестах, сгенерированных LLM, а специализированные агенты анализируют траектории выполнения и предлагают обновления. Вместо одной обвязки метод отбирает три варианта: lite (экономичный), medium (сбалансированный) и heavy (ориентированный на качество) с помощью нормализованного взвешенного отбора по квадратичному расстоянию. На бенчмарках Terminal-Bench 2, SWE-Bench Pro и ProgramBench режим heavy повышает pass-rate в среднем на 2.0 п.п. и снижает стоимость на задачу на 26.1% относительно RHO.", "emoji": "🧰", "title": "Эволюция обвязок AI-агентов без разметки: выше качество, ниже цена"}

Abstract Fréchet Forcing fine-tunes streaming video world models using self-generated rollouts and real-data statistics to prevent long-horizon drift without requiring teachers or long videos.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Streaming video world models generate the future chunk by chunk, conditioned on their own past and a stream of actions. Trained on short clips, they drift once a rollout exceeds the training horizon, as prediction errors re-enter the context and compound. A common remedy is to train the model on its own rollouts via distribution matching distillation (DMD). For action-conditioned world models, the teacher becomes a bottleneck: an external video model is agnostic to the actions, whereas the model's own pretrained network drifts in the same manner as the student. We observe that a long rollout need not match a teacher, provided it remains a realistic continuation of its own scene that follows the commanded actions. Realism and scene fidelity can be measured against real-data statistics, which remain valid at any horizon, while action following is preserved by the model's conditional generation loss. We propose Fréchet Forcing, which fine-tunes a world model together with its deployed multi-step sampler under three constraints. Generative anchoring applies the flow-matching loss after a self-generated history ending on real data. Context fidelity, a Fréchet distance (FD) on latent statistics of the free rollout centred on each clip's own ground truth, keeps every rollout faithful to its scene. Reality matching, an FD to real frames in a frozen perceptual space, keeps the generated frames realistic. Because all three targets are derived from short real clips without comparing a rollout against a long recorded future, training requires no teacher, no online critic and no long videos. Fine-tuning MiniWorld-1B, pretrained on 253-frame clips, for at most 36 GPU-hours on 381-frame clips yields rollouts that remain stable beyond 4,000 frames and continue to follow their actions in both robot manipulation and camera-controlled exploration.
[20.10.2026 05:41] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает метод Fréchet Forcing для дообучения потоковых video world models, которые генерируют видео чанк за чанком и склонны к дрейфу на длинных горизонтах. Вместо внешнего teacher-модели или online critic используется fine-tuning на собственных rollout'ах с тремя ограничениями: generative anchoring (flow-matching loss после самогенерированной истории, заканчивающейся реальными данными), context fidelity (Fréchet distance на латентных статистиках относительно ground truth клипа) и reality matching (FD к реальным кадрам в замороженном перцептивном пространстве). Такой подход не требует длинных видео и сохраняет следование действиям за счёт условной генерации. После дообучения MiniWorld-1B модель стабильно генерирует более 4000 кадров в задачах манипуляции роботом и исследования сцены камерой.", "emoji": "🎬", "title": "Стабильные миры на длинных горизонтах без учителя"}

Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Streaming video world models generate the future chunk by chunk, conditioned on their own past and a stream of actions. Trained on short clips, they drift once a rollout exceeds the training horizon, as prediction errors re-enter the context and compound. A common remedy is to train the model on its own rollouts via distribution matching distillation (DMD). For action-conditioned world models, the teacher becomes a bottleneck: an external video model is agnostic to the actions, whereas the model's own pretrained network drifts in the same manner as the student. We observe that a long rollout need not match a teacher, provided it remains a realistic continuation of its own scene that follows the commanded actions. Realism and scene fidelity can be measured against real-data statistics, which remain valid at any horizon, while action following is preserved by the model's conditional generation loss. We propose Fréchet Forcing, which fine-tunes a world model together with its deployed multi-step sampler under three constraints. Generative anchoring applies the flow-matching loss after a self-generated history ending on real data. Context fidelity, a Fréchet distance (FD) on latent statistics of the free rollout centred on each clip's own ground truth, keeps every rollout faithful to its scene. Reality matching, an FD to real frames in a frozen perceptual space, keeps the generated frames realistic. Because all three targets are derived from short real clips without comparing a rollout against a long recorded future, training requires no teacher, no online critic and no long videos. Fine-tuning MiniWorld-1B, pretrained on 253-frame clips, for at most 36 GPU-hours on 381-frame clips yields rollouts that remain stable beyond 4,000 frames and continue to follow their actions in both robot manipulation and camera-controlled exploration."

Abstract TCoVP introduces a spatiotemporally grounded video reasoning chain that links entities to timestamped visual evidence for segmentation, improving reasoning and mask prediction across benchmarks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Video Reasoning Segmentation (VRS) requires a model to identify an implicitly described target and predict its pixel-level mask trajectory, while resolving the spatial relations and temporal events that determine the answer. Text-only Chain-of-Thought (CoT) can explain a target choice without making the underlying visual references explicit, leaving a video reference gap between linguistic inference and the evidence distributed across frames. We introduce Temporal Chain of Video Primitives (TCoVP), a spatiotemporally grounded form of Video CoT that organizes query-relevant observations into a chronological reasoning chain. Each primitive associates an entity reference with temporal support and timestamped spatial evidence, and the chain interleaves these references with language to guide reasoning about which entity is relevant, when the queried event holds, and where its evidence appears. The selected target primitives then provide box prompts and temporal support for video mask prediction. We construct TCoVP-SFT-15k and TCoVP-RL-13k to learn this representation through supervised fine-tuning and chain-aligned reinforcement learning. Extensive experiments across five VRS benchmarks and three model backbones demonstrate the strong performance of TCoVP, highlighting the effectiveness of organizing spatial and temporal evidence within the reasoning process. All models and code will be publicly released. Project page: https://victor-wjw.github.io/TCoVP/
[20.10.2026 05:41] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен метод TCoVP, который организует рассуждения модели для задачи сегментации видео по языковому запросу (VRS). Вместо текстовой цепочки рассуждений (CoT) модель строит хронологическую цепочку видеопримитивов, где каждая сущность связана с временными метками и пространственными координатами на кадрах. Выбранные примитивы используются как box-промпты и временная опора для предсказания масок объектов. Обучение проводится через supervised fine-tuning на датасете TCoVP-SFT-15k и через reinforcement learning на TCoVP-RL-13k, а эксперименты на пяти бенчмарках подтверждают эффективность подхода.", "emoji": "🎬", "title": "Рассуждай по кадрам: видеоцепочка, привязанная ко времени и месту"}

Abstract DeOverlay uses source-anchored flow matching and visibility-normalized temporal aggregation to remove video overlays while preserving scene structure, and introduces DVTE-Bench for standardized evaluation.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Video overlay removal aims to recover scenes obscured by subtitles, titles, and logos while preserving surrounding content. Existing methods use temporal propagation or source-conditioned generation, but can leave residual overlays and distort scene structure when useful source information is obscured. We propose DeOverlay, a source-anchored flow model that reconstructs the clean video from the source latent with fixed source guidance. Specifically, we train a shared velocity network along paired source-to-clean paths using region-weighted flow matching, enabling successive cor- rections from intermediate states. To select local source cues, we develop visibility-normalized temporal aggregation that excludes masked positions and averages the remaining observations. Global source context and edit-region embeddings complement these cues through additive condi- tioning, retaining a single video-token sequence. To evaluate scene recovery across diverse overlay conditions, we introduce DVTE-Bench, comprising 1,631 paired videos across eight text-overlay categories, with OCR-screened clean targets, frame-aligned pixel masks, and a common evaluation protocol. Experiments on the complete release show that DeOverlay outperforms four baselines on all five reconstruction and perceptual metrics, reducing Mask LPIPS and Mask VFID by 58.3% and 70.3%, respectively, relative to the strongest evaluated baseline. Additional qualitative examples demonstrate the removal of graphic logos and stickers. We will make the complete benchmark, evaluation code, and test outputs availabl. Project page:https://yahooo-m.github.io/DVTE-Bench/
[20.10.2026 05:41] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет DeOverlay — модель на основе flow matching, которая убирает наложения (субтитры, титры, логотипы) из видео, восстанавливая исходную сцену. Модель опирается на source-anchored подход: обучает общую velocity network вдоль путей от исходного латентного представления к чистому видео с region-weighted flow matching. Для локальных подсказок используется visibility-normalized temporal aggregation, который игнорирует замаскированные пиксели и усредняет остальные наблюдения, а глобальный контекст добавляется через аддитивное кондиционирование. Также авторы выпускают бенчмарк DVTE-Bench из 1 631 пары видео, на котором DeOverlay превосходит четыре базовые модели по всем пяти метрикам, снижая Mask LPIPS и Mask VFID на 58,3% и 70,3%.", "emoji": "🎬", "title": "Убираем наложения и сохраняем сцену: чистое видео без субтитров и логотипов"}

To select local source cues, we develop visibility-normalized temporal aggregation that excludes masked positions and averages the remaining observations. Global source context and edit-region embeddings complement these cues through additive condi- tioning, retaining a single video-token sequence. To evaluate scene recovery across diverse overlay conditions, we introduce DVTE-Bench, comprising 1,631 paired videos across eight text-overlay categories, with OCR-screened clean targets, frame-aligned pixel masks, and a common evaluation protocol. Experiments on the complete release show that DeOverlay outperforms four baselines on all five reconstruction and perceptual metrics, reducing Mask LPIPS and Mask VFID by 58.3% and 70.3%, respectively, relative to the strongest evaluated baseline. Additional qualitative examples demonstrate the removal of graphic logos and stickers. We will make the complete benchmark, evaluation code, and test outputs availabl. Project page:https://yahooo-m.github.io/DVTE-Bench/"

Abstract SERA trains recursive language agents to generate evaluation rubrics for subtasks, improving decomposition, solving, and self-evaluation with reduced external supervision.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Recursive language-model agents decompose tasks and delegate subtasks to child instances of the same policy, forming a tree of work. Training them, however, is hard: the final outcome is verifiable, but the self-invented intermediate subtasks are numerous and carry no ground truth. Existing methods score each node with a verifier or judge, which is costly at scale and blind to decomposition quality. We argue that a recursive agent must learn three coupled capabilities within one set of weights: decomposing problems into subtasks, solving them, and evaluating the outcomes, each requiring its own training signal. SERA (Self-Evaluating Recursive Agents) turns evaluation into a learned capability of the policy itself. Before delegating, the parent writes a rubric of weighted success criteria for each child subtask; a ranking objective against verified outcomes then trains rubric generation so that the criteria track genuine subtask success. In addition, a complementary leaf-coverage signal provides direct credit for task decomposition. Our central finding is that training the policy to generate aligned rubrics is what drives the gains: because the same weights both evaluate and execute, learning to judge subtasks sharpens the agent's ability to solve them. Notably, external supervision is also reduced: the judge is consulted only to train the rubric generator, while solving is trained against the agent's own rubric scores, which outperform direct use of the judge. Beyond training, the learned rubric doubles as an inference-time selector for tree search. On TextCraft-Synth and TextWorld-Sync, SERA improves over strong recursive-agent baselines by 5.38 and 13.14 points on average, and rubric-guided tree search at inference adds a further 2.43 points on TextWorld-Sync.

Родительский агент перед делегированием пишет взвешенные критерии успеха для каждой дочерней подзадачи, а ranking-objective на основе проверенных результатов выравнивает эти критерии с реальным успехом. Дополнительный сигнал leaf-coverage напрямую поощряет качественную декомпозицию задачи, а внешний judge используется только для обучения генератора рубрик. Обученные рубрики также служат селектором при инференсе для tree search и дают прирост качества на TextCraft-Synth и TextWorld-Sync.", "emoji": "🌳", "title": "Агент, который сам учится судить о своих подзадачах"}
